{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that every Hodge-generic algebraic curve in $`\\mathcal A_2`$ over $`\\overline{\\mathbb Q}`$ contains only finitely many points whose abelian surface is isogenous to the square of an elliptic curve without complex multiplication (CM). Combining this result with the companion $`E\\times\\mathrm{CM}`$ and quaternionic-division finiteness theorems, we prove the curve case of Zilber–Pink in $`\\mathcal A_2`$ over $`\\overline{\\mathbb Q}`$, without a boundary hypothesis.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every Hodge-generic algebraic curve in $`\mathcal A_2`$ over $`\overline{\mathbb Q}`$ contains only finitely many points whose abelian surface is isogenous to the square of an elliptic curve without complex multiplication (CM). Combining this result with the companion $`E\times\mathrm{CM}`$ and quaternionic-division finiteness theorems, we prove the curve case of Zilber–Pink in $`\mathcal A_2`$ over $`\overline{\mathbb Q}`$, without a boundary hypothesis.

- **claim-elliptic-square-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-squares)): Let $C\subset\mathcal A_2$ be a nonempty reduced irreducible closed algebraic curve over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$. If $C$ is Hodge generic, then $\Sigma_{E^2}(C)$ is finite.
  - Explanation: A Hodge-generic algebraic curve contains only finitely many points whose abelian surface is isogenous to a non-CM elliptic square, allowing arbitrary principal polarizations and isogeny degrees.
- **claim-companion-branch-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-branches)): For every nonempty reduced irreducible closed Hodge-generic curve $C\subset\mathcal A_2$ over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, the following two subsets of $C(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ are finite:* 1. *the points for which $A_s$ is isogenous over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ to $E_1\times E_2$, with at least one elliptic factor having CM;* 2. *the points for which the full algebra $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}(A_s)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$ is an indefinite quaternion division algebra over $\mathbb Q$.* *These statements allow all CM fields, endomorphism orders, quaternion algebras, isogeny degrees and principal polarizations. They impose no boundary, reduction, integrality, height or field-degree condition.
  - Explanation: The companion results supply finiteness for products with a CM elliptic factor and for surfaces whose full rational endomorphism algebra is an indefinite quaternion division algebra.
- **claim-zilber-pink-curves** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-zp)): For every nonempty reduced irreducible closed Hodge-generic curve $C\subset\mathcal A_2$ over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, the set $$\Sigma(C)=C(\overline{\mathbb Q})\cap \bigcup_{\substack{Z\subset\mathcal A_2\ \mathrm{special}\mathstrut\\ \dim Z\le 1}} Z(\overline{\mathbb Q})$$ is finite.
  - Explanation: Combining the special-point theorem with the disjoint special-curve branches proves finiteness for intersections with all special points and special curves.
- **claim-common-parameter-height** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-int-height)): Fix an ambient dimension $N$, a bound $s_0$ for the number of equations, an integer $b$ bounding the degrees of the polynomial combinations below, and a real constant $C_0\geq2$. Fix also a finite list of algebraic regular germs and ordinary horizontal matrix germs on fixed algebraic curves over number fields. Dual and inverse matrices are allowed in this list. Then there is a constant $C$, depending only on these fixed data, with the following property.* *Let $d,h\geq2$ and let $z=(a,U)\in\mathbb A^N(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ with $a\ne0$. Suppose a weighted subcollection $V$ and $1\leq s\leq s_0$ equations $$\label{int:equations} E_\alpha(a,U)=\sum_{j\geq0}a^j e_{\alpha,j}(U)=0 \quad(1\leq\alpha\leq s)$$ satisfy the following conditions.* 1. *The point and the selected places satisfy $$\begin{aligned} h_{\mathrm{aff}}(z)&\leq C_0d^{C_0}h, &\|a\|_v&<1\quad(v\in V),\label{int:global-input}\\ \sum_{v\in V}w_v\log \|a\|_v^{-1} &\geq \frac{h}{C_0d^{C_0}}, &\sum_{v\in V}w_v\log^+\\|U\\|_v &\leq C_0d^{C_0}(1+\log h)^{C_0}. \label{int:local-input} \end{aligned}$$ Here $\\|U\\|_v$ is the maximum of the coordinate absolute values.* 2. *The coefficients are polynomials over a number field containing the target and equation data, with $$\label{int:coefficient-input} \deg e_{\alpha,j}\leq C_0(1+j),\qquad h_{\mathrm{aff}}(e_{\alpha,j}) \leq C_0(1+j+d)^{C_0}(1+\log h)^{C_0}.$$ The same equations are used at every selected embedding. They converge there at the target, and there are numbers $b_v\geq0$ such that, for all $\ell\geq0$ and all $\alpha$, $$\begin{aligned} \left\|\sum_{j\geq\ell}a^j e_{\alpha,j}(U)\right\|_v &\leq \|a\|_v^\ell\exp((\ell+1)b_v),\label{int:tail-input}\\ \sum_{v\in V}w_vb_v &\leq C_0d^{C_0}(1+\log h)^{C_0}. \end{aligned}$$* 3. *As analytic functions in the variables $(a,U)$, the $E_\alpha$ are polynomial combinations of degree at most $b$ of the coordinates and the fixed germs evaluated at $a$, and possibly at $aR$, where $R$ is one of the coordinates of $U$. Their constant coefficients may depend on the target. The algebraic germs are regular functions on the specified local curve charts. Thus every expression is also a convergent complex analytic germ near any finite point of $a=0$.* 4. *Let $W\subseteq\mathbb A^N_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}$ be any irreducible closed subvariety containing $z$ on which $a$ is nonconstant. For each prime divisor $D$ in its finite normalization lying above a component of $W\cap\{a=0\}$, at least one $E_\alpha$ is not identically zero as an analytic germ on the local branch at a general point of $D$.* *Then $h\leq Cd^C$.
  - Explanation: Fixed ordinary differential systems yield a polynomial height bound when common equations have controlled coefficients and tails, large proximity mass, small positive local coordinate logarithms, and nonidentity on every relevant algebraic boundary branch.
- **claim-fixed-fine-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-setup)): In proving [intro:squares](#intro:squares), we may work with a smooth geometrically irreducible curve $S$ over a fixed number field, a principally polarized abelian scheme $\mathscr A\to S$, and a nonconstant finite map from $S$ onto a dense open of the original curve $C$. We may equip the family with full symplectic levels at two distinct fixed primes at least three. Under the supposition that the required elliptic-square locus is infinite, we may fix a point $t_0\in S(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ in that locus.* *Further fixed finite covers, normalization, and removal of finite sets preserve this reduction. After enlarging a fixed field of definition $K$, there is a constant $b$ such that every selected lift $t$ of a retained coarse point $s$ satisfies $$\label{geo:degree-descent} [K(s):K]\leq [K(t):K]\leq b[K(s):K].$$ The smooth projective completion of $S$ and the divisor $[t_0]$ are available even when $C$ is complete.
  - Explanation: A fixed fine-level cover provides a smooth family, an interior elliptic-square center under the infinite-locus supposition, and bounded relative degrees for selected lifts. Fixed omissions affect only finitely many coarse points.
- **claim-orthogonal-operator-system** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-five-space)): The system $\mathcal P$ has rank five and a split nondegenerate trace pairing. Conjugation induces a surjective morphism $$\mathop{\mathrm{GSp}}_4\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(\mathcal P)$$ with scalar kernel. Its restriction to $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4$ is the spin covering, with kernel $\{1,-1\}$, and $\mathcal P$ is absolutely irreducible. For every odd prime $\ell$, the lattice $$\label{geo:integral-lattice} \Lambda_\ell= \{D\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\mathbb Z_\ell}(T_\ell\mathscr A_t): D^\dagger=D,\ \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(D)=0\}$$ is free of rank five with unimodular trace pairing.* *If $\mathscr A_t$ is isogenous to the square of a non-CM elliptic curve, then $$\label{geo:hodge-plane} F_t=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(\mathscr A_t)\cap\mathcal P_t$$ is a rational two-dimensional plane on which the trace pairing is positive definite. Here the intersection denotes Hodge endomorphisms in the Betti realization, equivalently actual rational endomorphisms in every realization.
  - Explanation: Trace-free symplectic-self-adjoint operators form a split quadratic system. Odd-prime integral lattices have unimodular trace pairing, while actual rational endomorphisms at a non-CM elliptic square form a positive plane.
- **claim-full-and-joint-monodromy** (classification, [source](#stmt-geo-monodromy)): The connected algebraic monodromy of $H$ is $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4$, and that of $\mathcal P$ is $\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}_5$. This remains true on every fixed finite cover, on a dense open, and for the conjugate families under consideration. The same connected monodromy is obtained by pullback to a smooth connected algebraic variety mapping dominantly to the base curve.* *Suppose a smooth connected algebraic variety $W$ maps dominantly to two such curves. The connected joint monodromy on the two five-dimensional systems is either $\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}_5\times\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}_5$, or the coarse joint image of $W$ is contained in a polarized isogeny correspondence. If only the first projection varies, its connected monodromy is still $\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}_5$.
  - Explanation: Full connected monodromy persists under fixed covers, conjugation, and dominant pullback. Joint monodromy is a product unless the coarse joint image lies in a polarized isogeny correspondence.
- **claim-finite-dominating-hecke-list** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-hecke-list)): For the fixed finite collection <a href="#geo:coarse-curves" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:coarse-curves">[geo:coarse-curves]</a>, only finitely many coarse Hecke correspondences admit a dominating relation between a pair of these curves. In particular there is a fixed finite list $\mathcal T$ containing every exceptional joint image in [geo:monodromy](#geo:monodromy). We may take this list closed under exchanging the two factors and choose, for every member, positive integral multipliers for isogenies realizing all its pairs. The list and these multipliers are fixed before any additional auxiliary levels are chosen.
  - Explanation: Only finitely many coarse correspondences dominate pairs in the fixed collection of conjugate curves. Their realizing multipliers are fixed before auxiliary levels are chosen; isolated isogenous pairs are not uniformly bounded by this assertion.
- **claim-finite-field-plane-positions** (construction, [source](#stmt-inc-finite-fields)): There is an absolute constant $q_0$ with the following property. Let $k$ be a finite field of odd cardinality $q>q_0$, let $H_0\subset V_k$ be a nondegenerate plane, and let $H\subset V_k$ be any plane. There are $g_-,g_+\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4(k)$ such that $$\dim(H_0+g_-H)=\dim(H_0+g_+H)=4,$$ the first sum is degenerate, and the second is nondegenerate. Here the action is the quadratic representation, so the assertion does not require surjectivity of $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}_4(k)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(V_k)$.
  - Explanation: Over sufficiently large odd finite fields, symplectic transformations move any target plane into positions giving either a degenerate or a nondegenerate independent sum with the fixed nondegenerate center plane.
- **claim-independent-auxiliary-markings** (construction, [source](#stmt-inc-markings)): After one fixed finite cover of the curve in [geo:setup](#geo:setup), there are distinct fixed odd primes $\ell_1,\ell_2$ and a fixed lift of the center $t_0$ with the following properties. Every non-CM elliptic-square target on the previous curve has a lift $t$ for which, in the respective marked five-spaces, $$\begin{aligned} \overline\Pi_{t_0,\ell_1}+\overline\Pi_{t,\ell_1} &\text{ is a degenerate four-space},\label{inc:first}\\ \overline\Pi_{t_0,\ell_2}+\overline\Pi_{t,\ell_2} &\text{ is a nondegenerate four-space}.\label{inc:second} \end{aligned}$$ The prime $\ell_1$ divides none of the fixed Hecke multipliers in [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list). The prime $\ell_2$ is different from every residue characteristic at which the fixed center is not potentially good. Both center-plane reductions are nondegenerate. The conditions remain valid upon simultaneous conjugation of all the data, and the degree of any chosen lift increases by at most a fixed factor.
  - Explanation: A connected fixed cover supports independent markings at the auxiliary primes. The degenerate incidence serves the sign obstruction, and the nondegenerate incidence serves exclusion of bad-center proximity.
- **claim-common-toric-torsion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inc-torus)): Let $X$ be one of the regular integral toroidal models just specified, let $\mathcal G\to X$ be its semiabelian family, and suppose its interior carries a full $\ell$-marking with $\ell$ invertible on $X$. Let $\bar z$ be a geometric point such that $\mathcal G_{\bar z}$ is a two-dimensional torus. Any two traits through $\bar z$ whose generic points lie in the interior have the same marked two-space $$W_{\bar z}\subset (\mathbb Z/\ell\mathbb Z)^4$$ coming from the special torus torsion. Along each trait it lifts to a saturated rank-two lattice $\mathcal T\subset T_\ell A$. Every actual integral endomorphism of the generic abelian variety, after extension of its field of definition, preserves $\mathcal T$. If $F\subset\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A)$ is a rational space of endomorphisms, the reduction of $(F\otimes\mathbb Q_\ell)\cap\Lambda_\ell$ therefore preserves $W_{\bar z}$ as well.
  - Explanation: Traits through the same toric specialization share a marked toric torsion subspace. Its saturated Tate lattice is preserved by actual integral endomorphisms and by integral operators in their rational spans.
- **claim-stabilizer-degeneracy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inc-stabilizer)): Let $V_4$ be a symplectic four-space over a field of characteristic different from two. No nondegenerate four-space of trace-free symplectic-self-adjoint operators can preserve a two-space $W\subset V_4$.
  - Explanation: A nondegenerate operator four-space cannot stabilize a symplectic two-space. The proof treats separately the nondegenerate and Lagrangian possibilities for the preserved subspace.
- **claim-bad-center-disk-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-inc-bad-disks)): Fix a finite place of the field of the center at which $A_{t_0}$ is not potentially good. There is a sufficiently small disk about the actual point $t_0$ in the completed local curve such that no selected target from [inc:markings](#inc:markings) lies in this disk. The radius is independent of the finite, possibly ramified extension over which a target is defined. The same conclusion holds for every conjugate of the fixed data, with a fixed finite collection of radii.
  - Explanation: The nondegenerate auxiliary incidence excludes selected targets from a fixed sufficiently small actual-center disk at every non-potentially-good center place, uniformly over ramified target extensions.
- **claim-small-full-endomorphisms** (bound, [source](#stmt-ht-endomorphisms)): Let $t$ be a target with $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}(\mathcal A_t)\otimes\mathbb Q\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\mathbb Q)$, and let $\mathcal O_t=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}(\mathcal A_t)$. For the principal Rosati involution $\dagger$, set $$Q_t(f)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{H_1}(ff^\dagger).$$ Then $$\label{ht:small-bounds} h_F(\mathcal A_t)\leq Ch,\qquad \|\mathop{\mathrm{disc}}\mathcal O_t\|\leq C(dh)^\kappa.$$ The discriminant here is that of the full geometric order for the trace of the regular representation of $\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\mathbb Q)$. There are integral endomorphisms $f_1,\ldots,f_4$ spanning $\mathcal O_t\otimes\mathbb Q$ and an independent labeled pair $\mathbf P=(P_1,P_2)$ spanning its Rosati-symmetric trace-free plane, all with $$\label{ht:rosati-small} Q_t(f_i),\ Q_t(P_j)\leq C(dh)^\kappa.$$ All geometric endomorphisms, and hence this pair and its de Rham coordinates, are defined over an extension $L/K(t)$ of bounded degree. The bound depends only on the dimension and the fixed data.
  - Explanation: Polynomial Rosati bounds control both a rational spanning set of the full geometric order and a labeled pair spanning its symmetric trace-free plane. The discriminant bound concerns the full order, and all endomorphisms are defined over a bounded relative extension.
- **claim-integral-biextension-lattice** (construction, [source](#stmt-ht-integral-model)): Let $T$ be a regular noetherian integral scheme, $U\subset T$ a dense open, and $\mathcal G\to T$ a smooth separated semiabelian scheme of relative dimension two with geometrically integral fibers. Suppose that $\mathcal G_U$ is an abelian scheme with principal polarization. The Poincaré bundle, viewed on $\mathcal G_U\times_U\mathcal G_U$ using that polarization, has a unique extension as a normalized biextension $\mathscr B$ on $\mathcal G\times_T\mathcal G$.* *This biextension defines a smooth vector-group extension $$\label{ht:vector-extension} 0\longrightarrow\omega_{\mathcal G/T} \longrightarrow\mathcal E\longrightarrow\mathcal G \longrightarrow0, \qquad \omega_{\mathcal G/T}=e^*\Omega^1_{\mathcal G/T},$$ where a locally free module denotes the additive group of its sections. The vector bundle $\mathscr H=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathcal E)$ has rank four and, on the characteristic-zero abelian open, identifies functorially with $H_1^{\rm dR}(\mathcal G_U/U)$.* *For any trait $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R\to T$ whose generic point belongs to that open, and any integral endomorphism $f$ of its generic abelian variety, the action of $f$ preserves the lattice $\mathscr H_R$. This remains true after every finite extension of traits on which $f$ is defined.
  - Explanation: A normalized Poincaré biextension extends over the regular semiabelian base and defines a vector extension whose Lie bundle realizes de Rham homology on the characteristic-zero abelian open. Actual integral endomorphisms preserve its trait lattices.
- **claim-finite-frame-costs** (bound, [source](#stmt-ht-frame-costs)): For each finite place let $\mathscr H_t$ be the lattice obtained from an available model above. There are nonnegative local costs $\delta_v(t)$, depending only on the fixed frames and models, such that the coefficients in the chosen $\mathcal P_{\rm dR}$ frame of any integral endomorphism $f\in\mathcal P_{{\rm dR},t}$ satisfy $$\label{ht:finite-frame-bound} \log^+\\|f\\|_{v,\mathrm{coord}}\leq\delta_v(t), \qquad \sum_{v\nmid\infty}n_v\delta_v(t)\leq Ch.$$ At sufficiently close places about a potentially-good center, the costs have bounded weighted sum. Outside a fixed finite set of rational primes they can be taken to be zero on those disks. These assertions are uniform over finite extensions and over the finitely many conjugate centers.
  - Explanation: Fixed denominators bound finite-place operator coordinates globally by the target height and locally near potentially-good centers by bounded weighted costs. Arbitrary finite extensions preserve the estimates.
- **claim-global-and-near-coordinates** (bound, [source](#stmt-ht-coordinates)): The labeled pair $\mathbf P$ of [ht:endomorphisms](#ht:endomorphisms), expressed in the fixed rational $\mathcal P_{\rm dR}$ frame, satisfies $$\label{ht:global-coordinates} h_{\rm aff}(\mathbf P)\leq C(h+\log d).$$ Let $V$ be any subcollection of sufficiently close places in the actual center branches, with bad-center places excluded as in [inc:bad-disks](#inc:bad-disks). Then $$\label{ht:near-coordinates} \sum_{v\in V}n_v\log\max\{1,\\|P_1\\|_{v,\mathrm{coord}}, \\|P_2\\|_{v,\mathrm{coord}}\} \leq C(1+\log h+\log d).$$ Both estimates hold for simultaneously conjugated data. They also hold for a fixed number of endomorphisms $F_j$ with $NF_j\in\mathcal O_t$ for a fixed positive integer $N$ and $Q_t(F_j)\leq C(dh)^\kappa$. If $\phi:\mathcal A_t\to\mathcal A_{t'}$ is a polarized isogeny of one of the fixed multipliers in [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list), write $I_t=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(\phi)$, so $I_t(D)=\phi D\phi^{-1}$. In particular the estimates hold for the transported pair $\widehat{\mathbf P}=I_t^{-1}\mathbf P'$. The added choices in this last assertion have bounded relative field degree.
  - Explanation: The global coordinate height may grow with the target height, but near-center positive coordinate logarithms grow only logarithmically. Fixed denominators and fixed-multiplier isogeny transport preserve these bounds.
- **claim-actual-branch-proximity-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-loc-mass)): For fixed constants $c>0$ and $C$, every selected target satisfies $$\label{loc:mass-estimate} \sum_{v\in V_0}w_v\log\frac1{\|a\|_v} \geq c h-C\bigl(1+\log(dh)\bigr)^2.$$ The constants are uniform over arbitrary ramification of the target fields. They may depend on the fixed disks and on $\mathcal S$.
  - Explanation: Selected targets retain proximity mass comparable to the ample height, with a logarithmic loss from convergence cutoffs. Only the actual center branch contributes, and ramification does not change the normalized estimate.
- **claim-rational-common-reduction-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-comparison)): At every retained finite-place embedding there is, after a finite local extension, a smooth proper abelian family over a smooth integral moduli chart such that $A_{t_0}$ and $A_t$ have the same polarized special fiber $\overline A$. Rational comparisons of their homologies with the cohomology of $\overline A$ identify their change of comparison on $\mathcal P$ with $Y(a)$. In particular, $$\label{loc:common-traces} \langle B_i,Y(a)P_j\rangle =\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\bigl(\overline B_i\,\overline P_j\bigr) \qquad(1\leq i,j\leq2).$$ Here the bars are actual specializations of the endomorphisms of the two lifted fibers. The same comparison is functorial for polarized isogenies with specified reductions. The assertion allows arbitrary ramification of the local field of $t$.
  - Explanation: At retained finite embeddings, rational cohomological comparison identifies horizontal back-transport with common-reduction endomorphism traces. The comparison is functorial and allows arbitrary ramification; integral preservation is established separately.
- **claim-fixed-center-isogeny-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-center-isogenies)): Fix two of the conjugate centers and one multiplier $m_*$ from [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list). There are finitely many characteristic-zero polarized isogenies of multiplier $m_*$ between these centers. After enlarging $\mathcal S$ by a fixed finite set, every polarized isogeny of that multiplier between their good special fibers is the specialization of one of these center isogenies. This assertion is simultaneous for the fixed finite list of centers and multipliers.
  - Explanation: For fixed centers and a fixed multiplier, only finitely many characteristic-zero polarized isogenies occur. Outside finitely many fixed primes, every special-fiber isogeny of that multiplier is one of their specializations.
- **claim-local-equation-types** (construction, [source](#stmt-loc-equations)): The nearby embeddings $V_0$ can be partitioned into groups using one of the following three systems, together with the base-coordinate equations in <a href="#loc:base-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:base-equations">[loc:base-equations]</a> below: $$\begin{aligned} \langle\mathbf B,Y(a)\mathbf P\rangle &=\mathbf r, &&\mathbf r\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_{2\times2}(\mathbb Q), \label{loc:single}\tag{1}\\ \langle\mathbf B,Y(a)\mathbf P\rangle &=\langle\mathbf B',Y'(aR)\mathbf P'\rangle, &&R=a'/a, \label{loc:paired}\tag{2}\\ \langle\mathbf B,Y(a)\mathbf P\rangle &=\langle I_0^{-1}\mathbf B',Y(a)\widehat{\mathbf P}\rangle, &&\widehat{\mathbf P}=I_t^{-1}\mathbf P'. \label{loc:exception}\tag{3} \end{aligned}$$ Here primes denote a simultaneous conjugate of the labeled data. Type <a href="#loc:single" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:single">[loc:single]</a> is used at infinity and at retained primes in $\mathcal S$. At the other retained primes, type <a href="#loc:paired" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:paired">[loc:paired]</a> is used if the coarse target pair lies outside the closed finite Hecke list of [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list), and type <a href="#loc:exception" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:exception">[loc:exception]</a> is used if it lies on that list. In types <a href="#loc:paired" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:paired">[loc:paired]</a> and <a href="#loc:exception" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:exception">[loc:exception]</a>, the following additional data and properties hold at every assigned embedding:* 1. *$\|a'\|_v=\|a\|_v$, hence $\|R\|_v=1$, and $h(R)\leq Ch$.* 2. *There are two actual common reductions $\overline A$ and $\overline A'$ of the unprimed and primed center–target pairs, respectively. Relative Frobenius $F:\overline A\longrightarrow\overline A'$ has polarized multiplier $p$ and conjugates both labeled pairs to their primed counterparts.* 3. *On each common reduction, smooth proper specialization at $\ell_1\ne p$ preserves the two separately saturated plane lattices. Their reductions span the independent degenerate four-space prescribed by [inc:markings](#inc:markings).* 4. *In type <a href="#loc:exception" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:exception">[loc:exception]</a>, $I_0=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(\phi_0)$ and $I_t=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(\phi_t)$ are conjugation isometries associated to polarized isogenies of the same fixed multiplier $m_*$. Their reductions satisfy $\overline\phi_0=\overline\phi_t$, and $$\label{loc:transport-square} I_0Y(a)=Y'(a')I_t.$$ Here $p\nmid\ell_1m_*$ and $\ell_1\nmid m_*$. The maps induced by $F$ and $\overline\phi_0$ on $\ell_1$-adic Tate modules are integral isomorphisms, up to their unit similitude multipliers.* *The entries of $\mathbf r$ have bounded denominators and logarithmic heights at most $C(1+\log h+\log d)$. The pairs $\widehat{\mathbf P}$ have fixed bounded denominators, polynomial Rosati norms, and all the coordinate bounds of [ht:coordinates](#ht:coordinates).
  - Explanation: Nearby embeddings are assigned common single, paired, or exceptional cross-pairing equations together with base equations. Frobenius eliminates varying residue characteristics from the paired equation; fixed center isogenies rewrite exceptional equations using a single transport.
- **claim-grouped-interpolation-data** (bound, [source](#stmt-loc-groups)): There are fixed constants $C$ such that either $h\leq Cd^C$, or there is one system of type <a href="#loc:single" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:single">[loc:single]</a>, <a href="#loc:paired" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:paired">[loc:paired]</a> or <a href="#loc:exception" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:exception">[loc:exception]</a>, with <a href="#loc:base-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:base-equations">[loc:base-equations]</a>, having the following properties. Its target is an algebraic point $z=(a,U)$ in an affine space of fixed dimension, and it holds at all embeddings in a weighted subcollection $V\subset V_0$. Writing each scalar expression as $$E(a,U)=\sum_{j\geq0}a^j e_j(U),$$ the numerical and function hypotheses of hold: $$\begin{aligned} h_{\mathrm{aff}}(z)&\leq Cd^C h, &\sum_{v\in V}w_v\log\frac1{\|a\|_v}&\geq\frac{h}{Cd^C}, \label{loc:group-height-mass}\\ \sum_{v\in V}w_v\log^+\\|U\\|_v &\leq Cd^C(1+\log h)^C, &\deg e_j&\leq C(1+j), \label{loc:group-local-degree}\\ h_{\mathrm{aff}}(e_j) &\leq C(1+j+d)^C(1+\log h)^C, \label{loc:coefficient-height}\\ \left\|\sum_{j\geq l}a^j e_j(U)\right\|_v &\leq \|a\|_v^l\exp\bigl((1+l)b_v\bigr),\notag\\ \sum_{v\in V}w_v b_v&\leq Cd^C(1+\log h)^C. \label{loc:tail-bound} \end{aligned}$$ Here $l\geq0$, $b_v\geq0$, and one common choice of $b_v$ works for the bounded number of expressions. Each expression is a polynomial combination of fixed bounded degree of the coordinates, fixed regular algebraic germs and fixed ordinary horizontal matrix germs, evaluated at $a$ and, in type <a href="#loc:paired" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:paired">[loc:paired]</a>, at $aR$. Only a fixed finite list of such germs is used. Constants in the expressions may depend on the target, with the bounds displayed above.* *There are at most $Cd^C$ groups in the construction. This bound counts ordered conjugate tuples, not elements of the Galois group of a normal closure. The proposition asserts no nonidentity condition on an algebraic subvariety through $z$.
  - Explanation: Either the height is already polynomially bounded, or a group of ordered conjugate tuples retains sufficient proximity mass and satisfies the numerical and fixed-function interpolation conditions. This proposition does not establish nonidentity.
- **claim-normalized-branch-continuation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-nid-continuation)): Let $W$ be an irreducible closed algebraic subvariety of the ambient affine space, containing $z$, on which $a$ is nonconstant. Let $D$ be a prime divisor on the normalization of $W$ lying over the generic point of a component of $W\cap\{a=0\}$. If every expression in $\mathscr E$ vanishes as a germ on this branch, then the following hold.* 1. *The base coordinates give actual algebraic projections to the fixed curve charts; the first projection $s$ is nonconstant and satisfies $x(s)=a$. In the paired case the second satisfies $x'(s')=aR$.* 2. *On a connected smooth algebraic open $W^\circ$ all the identities continue along every path. At a fixed point of $W^\circ$, continuation around a loop changes the horizontal transports by their pullback monodromy, while returning the algebraic coordinates to their original values.* 3. *Every operator pair that is independent at $z$ is independent at a general point of $W^\circ$.* *The same conclusions hold if the pullbacks of all expressions vanish to infinite order along $D$.
  - Explanation: An identity on a normalized boundary branch extends to a connected smooth algebraic open. Base equations recover actual curve projections, monodromy changes only horizontal transports, and pairs independent at the target remain generically independent.
- **claim-orthogonal-matrix-span** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nid-matrix-span)): Let $V$ be the complex five-dimensional quadratic representation of [geo:five-space](#geo:five-space), and let $G=\mathop{\mathrm{SO}}(V)$.* 1. *If $b,v\in V$ are nonzero, the function $g% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}% \mapsto\EndAccSupp{}\langle b,gv\rangle$ on $G$ is not constant.* 2. *The complex linear span of $G$ is $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\mathbb C}(V)$. Consequently, if $b,c\in V$, $p,q\in V_1$, and $M_0:V_1\to V$ is an isomorphism, the identities $$\langle b,gM_0p\rangle=\langle c,gM_0q\rangle\qquad(g\in G)$$ imply $b\otimes p=c\otimes q$, where the first tensor factors are identified with their duals by the quadratic pairing.* *The assertions remain valid if the identities are initially known only on a Zariski-dense monodromy subgroup.
  - Explanation: Nonzero matrix coefficients of the orthogonal representation are nonconstant, and the group spans the full matrix algebra. Pairing identities therefore force tensor equalities, including when initially known only on dense monodromy.
- **claim-frobenius-sign-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nid-frobenius)): In an exceptional group of [loc:equations](#loc:equations), the target data cannot satisfy, for either common sign $\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}$, $$\label{nid:sign-equalities} I_0^{-1}B'_i=\epsilon B_i\quad(i=1,2), \qquad I_t^{-1}P'_j=\epsilon P_j\quad(j=1,2)$$ as equalities of rational endomorphisms. Here $I_0$ and $I_t$ are conjugation by the fixed-multiplier polarized isogenies specified in [loc:equations](#loc:equations).
  - Explanation: Neither common sign can identify the transported center and target pairs. The positive sign contradicts the Frobenius multiplier being a rational square, while the negative sign contradicts the degenerate reduction of the separately saturated planes.
- **claim-universal-branch-nonidentity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nid-nonidentity)): For every group selected in [loc:groups](#loc:groups), every irreducible closed algebraic $W$ containing its target and having nonconstant $a$, and every normalized branch over the generic point of a component of $W\cap\{a=0\}$, at least one of its equations is a nonzero germ on that branch. This holds for each of the three types <a href="#loc:single" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:single">[loc:single]</a>, <a href="#loc:paired" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:paired">[loc:paired]</a>, and <a href="#loc:exception" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:exception">[loc:exception]</a>, with their base-coordinate equations included.
  - Explanation: Every selected equation system has a nonzero germ on every relevant normalized branch through an arbitrary algebraic variety containing the target. Operator coordinates away from the target need not be Hodge classes.
- **claim-selected-lift-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-nid-height)): There are constants $C>0$ and $\kappa>0$, depending only on the fixed curve, covers, center, and auxiliary data, such that every selected non-CM elliptic-square lift $t$ satisfies $$\label{nid:height-bound} h(t)\le C\max\{2,[K(t):K]\}^{\kappa}.$$ The height is the shifted ample height of [ht:conventions](#ht:conventions).
  - Explanation: The grouped numerical bounds and universal branch nonidentity give a polynomial height bound for all selected non-CM elliptic-square lifts, with constants depending only on the fixed geometric and auxiliary data.
- **claim-full-order-galois-orbit** (bound, [source](#stmt-fin-orbit)): Let $C\subset\mathcal A_2$ be a curve as in [intro:squares](#intro:squares). There are a number field $K$ defining $C$ and constants $c,\delta>0$ such that every $s\in\Sigma_{E^2}(C)$ satisfies $$\label{fin:orbit-bound} \#\bigl(\operatorname{Aut}(\mathbb C/K)\cdot s\bigr) =[K(s):K]\ \geq\ c\Delta_s^\delta.$$ The constants are uniform over the whole indicated locus.
  - Explanation: Fixed-cover degree descent and the full-order discriminant bound convert the selected-lift height theorem into a uniform polynomial lower bound for coarse-point Galois orbits. Finite omissions are restored.
- **claim-square-locus-and-complexity** (classification, [source](#stmt-fin-squares)): An algebraic point $s\in\mathcal A_2(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ is isogenous over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ to $E^2$ with $E$ non-CM if and only if it is Hodge generic on an $E^2$-special curve. This statement allows the given arbitrary principal polarization on $A_s$ and an arbitrary unpolarized isogeny. At such a point, the complexity $\Delta(Z_s)$ of its special curve in (Daw and Orr 2022, sec. 6.3) [[DawOrr2022](#ref-DawOrr2022)] equals $\Delta_s$.
  - Explanation: The entire unpolarized non-CM square locus equals the Hodge-generic locus of elliptic-square special curves. Using the actual principal polarization and homology lattice identifies the special-curve complexity with the full geometric order discriminant.
- **claim-special-curve-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-fin-classification)): Let $Z\subset\mathcal A_2$ be a special curve and let $s\in Z(\overline{\mathbb Q})$ be Hodge generic on $Z$. Exactly one of the following holds:* 1. *$A_s$ is isogenous to $E\times F$, where $E$ is non-CM and $F$ is CM;* 2. *$A_s$ is isogenous to $E^2$, where $E$ is non-CM;* 3. *the full algebra $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}^0(A_s)$ is an indefinite quaternion division algebra over $\mathbb Q$.* *Every point of a special curve that is not Hodge generic on it is a special point.
  - Explanation: Hodge-generic points on special curves belong to exactly one of the product-with-CM, non-CM square, or quaternion-division branches. Nongeneric points on such curves are special points.
- **claim-special-closure-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fin-special-closure)): Let $C\subset\mathcal A_2$ be a nonempty reduced irreducible closed algebraic curve over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, and let $S_C$ be the smallest Shimura-special subvariety containing $C$. Then $$C(\overline{\mathbb Q})\cap \bigcup_{\substack{Z\subset\mathcal A_2\ \mathrm{special}\mathstrut\\ \dim Z<\dim S_C-1}} Z(\overline{\mathbb Q})$$ is finite.
  - Explanation: For an arbitrary algebraic curve, finiteness holds below the dimension threshold determined by its smallest special closure. The proof uses the full theorem or special-point finiteness, with an empty locus when the special closure is a curve.
- **claim-fixed-variable-effective-algebra** (bound, [source](#stmt-int-algebra)): Fix $M$. Suppose at most $D$ polynomials in $M$ variables have degree at most $D\geq2$ and affine coefficient height at most $H$.* 1. *Their ideal has a degree-compatible Gröbner basis of polynomially bounded degree and coefficient height at most $\operatorname{poly}_M(D)(1+H)$. Each basis element has a representation in the original generators whose degree and coefficient height satisfy the same types of bounds. Normal-form reduction can be chosen linear. For an input of degree $T$, its representation by the remainder and the original generators has degree at most $T+\Delta$, where $\Delta=\operatorname{poly}_M(D)$ is independent of $T$.* 2. *If a polynomial of degree $T$ belongs to the ideal, it has a certificate of membership of degree polynomial in $D+T$. The coefficient height of a certificate can be bounded polynomially in $D+T$ times one plus the heights of the given polynomial and generators.* 3. *Every irreducible component of the common zero set has degree polynomial in $D$, and admits set-theoretic defining equations whose number and degrees are polynomial in $D$ and whose affine coefficient heights are at most $\operatorname{poly}_M(D)(1+H)$.* *All bounds are absolute and are unchanged by enlarging the number field. The ideals in <span class="upright">(a)</span> and <span class="upright">(b)</span> need not be radical.
  - Explanation: With the number of variables fixed, polynomial bounds control Gröbner bases, membership certificates, and equations of irreducible components using absolute logarithmic heights. The ideal-membership assertions allow nonradical ideals.
- **claim-uniform-boundary-truncation** (construction, [source](#stmt-int-multiplicity)): Use the fixed function data of [int:height](#int:height). There is a polynomial $M_0(D)$, depending only on those data and $N$, with the following property. Let $W\subseteq\mathbb A^N$ be irreducible of degree at most $D$, with $a$ nonconstant, and suppose its normalized boundary branches satisfy condition <span class="upright">(iv)</span>. There are integers $0\leq c_\alpha\leq M_0(D)$ and an integer $1\leq m\leq M_0(D)$ such that, for $$E=\sum_{\alpha=1}^s c_\alpha E_\alpha =\sum_{j\geq0}a^j e_j(U), \qquad E_{<m}=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}a^je_j(U),$$ every prime divisor $D_0$ of the normalization over $W\cap\{a=0\}$ satisfies $$\label{int:strict-order} \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_0}E_{<m}<m\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{D_0}a.$$ The constants do not depend on the coefficient sizes of $W$ or of the polynomial combinations defining $E_\alpha$.
  - Explanation: Ordinary differential multiplicity on varying algebraic slices produces a bounded integer combination and truncation having a pole after division by the corresponding power of the common parameter on every normalized boundary divisor.
- **claim-rational-graph-boundary** (bound, [source](#stmt-int-graph)): Let $W$ be an irreducible affine variety of dimension $k\geq1$, and let $a$ be a nonconstant regular coordinate. Suppose a polynomial $F$ and an integer $m\geq1$ satisfy $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_D F<m\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_Da$$ on every normalized divisor above $W\cap\{a=0\}$. Let $W'$ be the affine closure of the graph $$q=g:=a^{-m}F\quad\text{over }W\setminus\{a=0\},$$ and let $\pi:W'\to W$ be the projection. Then $$\label{int:projected-boundary} \dim\overline{\pi(W'\cap\{a=0\})}\leq k-2.$$ In particular, for any polynomial $f(U)$, $$\label{int:graph-cut} \dim\bigl(W'\cap\{a=0, q+f(U)=0\}\bigr)\leq k-2.$$ When $k=1$ these sets are empty. Degrees and heights of $W'$ admit the bounds of [int:algebra](#int:algebra) in terms of defining equations of $W$, $F$, and $m$.
  - Explanation: If the rational graph function has a pole on every normalized boundary divisor, its projected boundary has dimension at most the stated lower threshold. Fixing the graph coordinate gives the same bound for the indicated incidence, without bounding the entire graph boundary.
- **claim-layered-actual-ideal-interpolation** (construction, [source](#stmt-int-auxiliary)): Fix $M$. In $\overline{\mathbb Q}[a,U,q]$, with $M$ variables in total, let $I=(I_1,\ldots,I_r)$ be generated by polynomials of controlled number, degree, and height. Suppose its zero set $W'$ is irreducible of dimension $k\geq1$. Let $$f_0=q+e_m(U),\qquad J=(I,a,f_0),\qquad F_n=f_0+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a^j e_{m+j}(U),$$ where $\deg e_{m+j}\leq C_0(1+m+j)$ and $\dim V(J)\leq k-2$, interpreted as emptiness for $k=1$. Let $D\geq2$ bound $r$, $m$, the generator degrees, and $C_0$. There are constants $B,B'$, polynomial in $D$ for fixed $M$, such that the following holds. Whenever $n,L\geq1$ and $$\label{int:condition-count} B'n(1+L+Bn+B)^{k-2}<\binom{L+k}{k},$$ there is a polynomial $P$ with $\deg P\leq L$, nonzero on $W'$, and $$\label{int:actual-membership} P\in(I,a^n,F_n).$$ For $k=1$ the left side of <a href="#int:condition-count" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:condition-count">[int:condition-count]</a> is replaced by zero. Both $P$ and a certificate for <a href="#int:actual-membership" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:actual-membership">[int:actual-membership]</a> can have degree polynomial in $D+L+n$ and coefficient height polynomial in $D+L+n$ times one plus the coefficient heights of $I_1,\ldots,I_r,e_m,\ldots,e_{m+n-1}$.* *In particular, for any prescribed positive integer $c$, one can take $n=cL$ with $L$ polynomial in $D+c$.
  - Explanation: Layered linear normal-form conditions produce a polynomial nonzero on the graph variety in the actual ideal generated by its equations, a high parameter power, and the truncated tail. The dimension saving permits any prescribed positive integral order-to-degree ratio.

          "exact_quote": "2.  *The coefficients are polynomials over a number field containing the target and equation data, with $$\\label{int:coefficient-input}\n     \\deg e_{\\alpha,j}\\leq C_0(1+j),\\qquad\n     h_{\\mathrm{aff}}(e_{\\alpha,j})\n      \\leq C_0(1+j+d)^{C_0}(1+\\log h)^{C_0}.$$ The same equations are used at every selected embedding. They converge there at the target, and there are numbers $b_v\\geq0$ such that, for all $\\ell\\geq0$ and all $\\alpha$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     \\left|\\sum_{j\\geq\\ell}a^j e_{\\alpha,j}(U)\\right|_v\n       &\\leq |a|_v^\\ell\\exp((\\ell+1)b_v),\\label{int:tail-input}\\\\\n     \\sum_{v\\in V}w_vb_v\n       &\\leq C_0d^{C_0}(1+\\log h)^{C_0}.\n    \\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-016.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-int-height",
          "exact_quote": "As analytic functions in the variables $(a,U)$, the $E_\\alpha$ are polynomial combinations of degree at most $b$ of the coordinates and the fixed germs evaluated at $a$, and possibly at $aR$, where $R$ is one of the coordinates of $U$. Their constant coefficients may depend on the target. The algebraic germs are regular functions on the specified local curve charts. Thus every expression is also a convergent complex analytic germ near any finite point of $a=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.4"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-setup",
        "exact_quote": "In proving [intro:squares](#intro:squares), we may work with a smooth geometrically irreducible curve $S$ over a fixed number field, a principally polarized abelian scheme $\\mathscr A\\to S$, and a nonconstant finite map from $S$ onto a dense open of the original curve $C$. We may equip the family with full symplectic levels at two distinct fixed primes at least three. Under the supposition that the required elliptic-square locus is infinite, we may fix a point $t_0\\in S(\\overline{\\mathbb Q})$ in that locus.*\n\n\n*Further fixed finite covers, normalization, and removal of finite sets preserve this reduction. After enlarging a fixed field of definition $K$, there is a constant $b$ such that every selected lift $t$ of a retained coarse point $s$ satisfies $$\\label{geo:degree-descent}\n [K(s):K]\\leq [K(t):K]\\leq b[K(s):K].$$ The smooth projective completion of $S$ and the divisor $[t_0]$ are available even when $C$ is complete.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-geo-five-space",
        "exact_quote": "The system $\\mathcal P$ has rank five and a split nondegenerate trace pairing.         "anchor": "stmt-geo-monodromy",
        "exact_quote": "The connected algebraic monodromy of $H$ is $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_4$, and that of $\\mathcal P$ is $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}_5$. This remains true on every fixed finite cover, on a dense open, and for the conjugate families under consideration. The same connected monodromy is obtained by pullback to a smooth connected algebraic variety mapping dominantly to the base curve.*\n\n*Suppose a smooth connected algebraic variety $W$ maps dominantly to two such curves.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-finite-fields",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $q_0$ with the following property. Let $k$ be a finite field of odd cardinality $q>q_0$, let $H_0\\subset V_k$ be a nondegenerate plane, and let $H\\subset V_k$ be any plane. There are $g_-,g_+\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_4(k)$ such that $$\\dim(H_0+g_-H)=\\dim(H_0+g_+H)=4,$$ the first sum is degenerate, and the second is nondegenerate. Here the action is the quadratic representation, so the assertion does not require surjectivity of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Sp}}_4(k)\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}(V_k)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"

        "anchor": "stmt-inc-markings",
        "exact_quote": "After one fixed finite cover of the curve in [geo:setup](#geo:setup), there are distinct fixed odd primes $\\ell_1,\\ell_2$ and a fixed lift of the center $t_0$ with the following properties. Every non-CM elliptic-square target on the previous curve has a lift $t$ for which, in the respective marked five-spaces, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\overline\\Pi_{t_0,\\ell_1}+\\overline\\Pi_{t,\\ell_1}\n   &\\text{ is a degenerate four-space},\\label{inc:first}\\\\\n \\overline\\Pi_{t_0,\\ell_2}+\\overline\\Pi_{t,\\ell_2}\n   &\\text{ is a nondegenerate four-space}.\\label{inc:second}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The prime $\\ell_1$ divides none of the fixed Hecke multipliers in [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list).       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-torus",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be one of the regular integral toroidal models just specified, let $\\mathcal G\\to X$ be its semiabelian family, and suppose its interior carries a full $\\ell$-marking with $\\ell$ invertible on $X$. Let $\\bar z$ be a geometric point such that $\\mathcal G_{\\bar z}$ is a two-dimensional torus. Any two traits through $\\bar z$ whose generic points lie in the interior have the same marked two-space $$W_{\\bar z}\\subset (\\mathbb Z/\\ell\\mathbb Z)^4$$ coming from the special torus torsion. Along each trait it lifts to a saturated rank-two lattice $\\mathcal T\\subset T_\\ell A$. Every actual integral endomorphism of the generic abelian variety, after extension of its field of definition, preserves $\\mathcal T$. If $F\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}^0(A)$ is a rational space of endomorphisms, the reduction of $(F\\otimes\\mathbb Q_\\ell)\\cap\\Lambda_\\ell$ therefore preserves $W_{\\bar z}$ as well.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },

      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be one of the regular integral toroidal models just specified, let $\\mathcal G\\to X$ be its semiabelian family, and suppose its interior carries a full $\\ell$-marking with $\\ell$ invertible on $X$. Let $\\bar z$ be a geometric point such that $\\mathcal G_{\\bar z}$ is a two-dimensional torus. Any two traits through $\\bar z$ whose generic points lie in the interior have the same marked two-space $$W_{\\bar z}\\subset (\\mathbb Z/\\ell\\mathbb Z)^4$$ coming from the special torus torsion. Along each trait it lifts to a saturated rank-two lattice $\\mathcal T\\subset T_\\ell A$. Every actual integral endomorphism of the generic abelian variety, after extension of its field of definition, preserves $\\mathcal T$. If $F\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}^0(A)$ is a rational space of endomorphisms, the reduction of $(F\\otimes\\mathbb Q_\\ell)\\cap\\Lambda_\\ell$ therefore preserves $W_{\\bar z}$ as well.",
      "explanation": "Traits through the same toric specialization share a marked toric torsion subspace. Its saturated Tate lattice is preserved by actual integral endomorphisms and by integral operators in their rational spans.",

There are integral endomorphisms $f_1,\\ldots,f_4$ spanning $\\mathcal O_t\\otimes\\mathbb Q$ and an independent labeled pair $\\mathbf P=(P_1,P_2)$ spanning its Rosati-symmetric trace-free plane, all with $$\\label{ht:rosati-small}\n Q_t(f_i),\\ Q_t(P_j)\\leq C(dh)^\\kappa.$$ All geometric endomorphisms, and hence this pair and its de Rham coordinates, are defined over an extension $L/K(t)$ of bounded degree.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ht-integral-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a regular noetherian integral scheme, $U\\subset T$ a dense open, and $\\mathcal G\\to T$ a smooth separated semiabelian scheme of relative dimension two with geometrically integral fibers. Suppose that $\\mathcal G_U$ is an abelian scheme with principal polarization. The Poincaré bundle, viewed on $\\mathcal G_U\\times_U\\mathcal G_U$ using that polarization, has a unique extension as a normalized biextension $\\mathscr B$ on $\\mathcal G\\times_T\\mathcal G$.*\n\n\n*This biextension defines a smooth vector-group extension $$\\label{ht:vector-extension}\n 0\\longrightarrow\\omega_{\\mathcal G/T}\n \\longrightarrow\\mathcal E\\longrightarrow\\mathcal G\n \\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad \\omega_{\\mathcal G/T}=e^*\\Omega^1_{\\mathcal G/T},$$ where a locally free module denotes the additive group of its sections. The vector bundle $\\mathscr H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(\\mathcal E)$ has rank four and, on the characteristic-zero abelian open, identifies functorially with $H_1^{\\rm dR}(\\mathcal G_U/U)$.*\n\n*For any trait $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R\\to T$ whose generic point belongs to that open, and any integral endomorphism $f$ of its generic abelian variety, the action of $f$ preserves the lattice $\\mathscr H_R$. This remains true after every finite extension of traits on which $f$ is defined.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },

The vector bundle $\\mathscr H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lie}}(\\mathcal E)$ has rank four and, on the characteristic-zero abelian open, identifies functorially with $H_1^{\\rm dR}(\\mathcal G_U/U)$.*\n\n*For any trait $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R\\to T$ whose generic point belongs to that open, and any integral endomorphism $f$ of its generic abelian variety, the action of $f$ preserves the lattice $\\mathscr H_R$.         "anchor": "stmt-ht-frame-costs",
        "exact_quote": "For each finite place let $\\mathscr H_t$ be the lattice obtained from an available model above. There are nonnegative local costs $\\delta_v(t)$, depending only on the fixed frames and models, such that the coefficients in the chosen $\\mathcal P_{\\rm dR}$ frame of any integral endomorphism $f\\in\\mathcal P_{{\\rm dR},t}$ satisfy $$\\label{ht:finite-frame-bound}\n \\log^+\\|f\\|_{v,\\mathrm{coord}}\\leq\\delta_v(t),\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{v\\nmid\\infty}n_v\\delta_v(t)\\leq Ch.$$ At sufficiently close places about a potentially-good center, the costs have bounded weighted sum.         "exact_quote": "The labeled pair $\\mathbf P$ of [ht:endomorphisms](#ht:endomorphisms), expressed in the fixed rational $\\mathcal P_{\\rm dR}$ frame, satisfies $$\\label{ht:global-coordinates}\n h_{\\rm aff}(\\mathbf P)\\leq C(h+\\log d).$$ Let $V$ be any subcollection of sufficiently close places in the actual center branches, with bad-center places excluded as in [inc:bad-disks](#inc:bad-disks). Then $$\\label{ht:near-coordinates}\n \\sum_{v\\in V}n_v\\log\\max\\{1,\\|P_1\\|_{v,\\mathrm{coord}},\n                                  \\|P_2\\|_{v,\\mathrm{coord}}\\}\n       \\leq C(1+\\log h+\\log d).$$ Both estimates hold for simultaneously conjugated data. They also hold for a fixed number of endomorphisms $F_j$ with $NF_j\\in\\mathcal O_t$ for a fixed positive integer $N$ and $Q_t(F_j)\\leq C(dh)^\\kappa$. If $\\phi:\\mathcal A_t\\to\\mathcal A_{t'}$ is a polarized isogeny of one of the fixed multipliers in [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list), write $I_t=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}(\\phi)$, so $I_t(D)=\\phi D\\phi^{-1}$.       "explanation": "Selected targets retain proximity mass comparable to the ample height, with a logarithmic loss from convergence cutoffs. Only the actual center branch contributes, and ramification does not change the normalized estimate.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "At every retained finite-place embedding there is, after a finite local extension, a smooth proper abelian family over a smooth integral moduli chart such that $A_{t_0}$ and $A_t$ have the same polarized special fiber $\\overline A$. Rational comparisons of their homologies with the cohomology of $\\overline A$ identify their change of comparison on $\\mathcal P$ with $Y(a)$. In particular, $$\\label{loc:common-traces}\n \\langle B_i,Y(a)P_j\\rangle\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\bigl(\\overline B_i\\,\\overline P_j\\bigr)\n \\qquad(1\\leq i,j\\leq2).$$ Here the bars are actual specializations of the endomorphisms of the two lifted fibers. The same comparison is functorial for polarized isogenies with specified reductions. The assertion allows arbitrary ramification of the local field of $t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-loc-comparison",

In particular, $$\\label{loc:common-traces}\n \\langle B_i,Y(a)P_j\\rangle\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\bigl(\\overline B_i\\,\\overline P_j\\bigr)\n \\qquad(1\\leq i,j\\leq2).$$ Here the bars are actual specializations of the endomorphisms of the two lifted fibers. The same comparison is functorial for polarized isogenies with specified reductions. The assertion allows arbitrary ramification of the local field of $t$.",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-center-isogenies",
        "exact_quote": "Fix two of the conjugate centers and one multiplier $m_*$ from [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list). There are finitely many characteristic-zero polarized isogenies of multiplier $m_*$ between these centers. After enlarging $\\mathcal S$ by a fixed finite set, every polarized isogeny of that multiplier between their good special fibers is the specialization of one of these center isogenies. This assertion is simultaneous for the fixed finite list of centers and multipliers.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-loc-center-isogenies",

          "source_id": "PM-016.4"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-equations",
        "exact_quote": "The nearby embeddings $V_0$ can be partitioned into groups using one of the following three systems, together with the base-coordinate equations in <a href=\"#loc:base-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:base-equations\">[loc:base-equations]</a> below: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\langle\\mathbf B,Y(a)\\mathbf P\\rangle\n   &=\\mathbf r,\n   &&\\mathbf r\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mat}}_{2\\times2}(\\mathbb Q),\n   \\label{loc:single}\\tag{1}\\\\\n \\langle\\mathbf B,Y(a)\\mathbf P\\rangle\n   &=\\langle\\mathbf B',Y'(aR)\\mathbf P'\\rangle,\n   &&R=a'/a,\n   \\label{loc:paired}\\tag{2}\\\\\n \\langle\\mathbf B,Y(a)\\mathbf P\\rangle\n   &=\\langle I_0^{-1}\\mathbf B',Y(a)\\widehat{\\mathbf P}\\rangle,\n   &&\\widehat{\\mathbf P}=I_t^{-1}\\mathbf P'.\n   \\label{loc:exception}\\tag{3}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here primes denote a simultaneous conjugate of the labeled data. Type <a href=\"#loc:single\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:single\">[loc:single]</a> is used at infinity and at retained primes in $\\mathcal S$. At the other retained primes, type <a href=\"#loc:paired\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:paired\">[loc:paired]</a> is used if the coarse target pair lies outside the closed finite Hecke list of [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list), and type <a href=\"#loc:exception\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:exception\">[loc:exception]</a> is used if it lies on that list. In types <a href=\"#loc:paired\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:paired\">[loc:paired]</a> and <a href=\"#loc:exception\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:exception\">[loc:exception]</a>, the following additional data and properties hold at every assigned embedding:*\n\n1.  *$|a'|_v=|a|_v$, hence $|R|_v=1$, and $h(R)\\leq Ch$.*\n\n2.  *There are two actual common reductions $\\overline A$ and $\\overline A'$ of the unprimed and primed center–target pairs, respectively. Relative Frobenius $F:\\overline A\\longrightarrow\\overline A'$ has polarized multiplier $p$ and conjugates both labeled pairs to their primed counterparts.*\n\n3.  *On each common reduction, smooth proper specialization at $\\ell_1\\ne p$ preserves the two separately saturated plane lattices. Their reductions span the independent degenerate four-space prescribed by [inc:markings](#inc:markings).*\n\n\n4.  *In type <a href=\"#loc:exception\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:exception\">[loc:exception]</a>, $I_0=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}(\\phi_0)$ and $I_t=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}(\\phi_t)$ are conjugation isometries associated to polarized isogenies of the same fixed multiplier $m_*$. Their reductions satisfy $\\overline\\phi_0=\\overline\\phi_t$, and $$\\label{loc:transport-square}\n     I_0Y(a)=Y'(a')I_t.$$ Here $p\\nmid\\ell_1m_*$ and $\\ell_1\\nmid m_*$. The maps induced by $F$ and $\\overline\\phi_0$ on $\\ell_1$-adic Tate modules are integral isomorphisms, up to their unit similitude multipliers.*\n\n*The entries of $\\mathbf r$ have bounded denominators and logarithmic heights at most $C(1+\\log h+\\log d)$. The pairs $\\widehat{\\mathbf P}$ have fixed bounded denominators, polynomial Rosati norms, and all the coordinate bounds of [ht:coordinates](#ht:coordinates).",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "explanation": "Nearby embeddings are assigned common single, paired, or exceptional cross-pairing equations together with base equations.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-groups",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed constants $C$ such that either $h\\leq Cd^C$, or there is one system of type <a href=\"#loc:single\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:single\">[loc:single]</a>, <a href=\"#loc:paired\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:paired\">[loc:paired]</a> or <a href=\"#loc:exception\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:exception\">[loc:exception]</a>, with <a href=\"#loc:base-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:base-equations\">[loc:base-equations]</a>, having the following properties. Its target is an algebraic point $z=(a,U)$ in an affine space of fixed dimension, and it holds at all embeddings in a weighted subcollection $V\\subset V_0$. Writing each scalar expression as $$E(a,U)=\\sum_{j\\geq0}a^j e_j(U),$$ the numerical and function hypotheses of hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n h_{\\mathrm{aff}}(z)&\\leq Cd^C h,\n &\\sum_{v\\in V}w_v\\log\\frac1{|a|_v}&\\geq\\frac{h}{Cd^C},\n \\label{loc:group-height-mass}\\\\\n \\sum_{v\\in V}w_v\\log^+\\|U\\|_v\n   &\\leq Cd^C(1+\\log h)^C,\n &\\deg e_j&\\leq C(1+j),\n \\label{loc:group-local-degree}\\\\\n h_{\\mathrm{aff}}(e_j)\n   &\\leq C(1+j+d)^C(1+\\log h)^C,\n \\label{loc:coefficient-height}\\\\\n \\left|\\sum_{j\\geq l}a^j e_j(U)\\right|_v\n   &\\leq |a|_v^l\\exp\\bigl((1+l)b_v\\bigr),\\notag\\\\\n \\sum_{v\\in V}w_v b_v&\\leq Cd^C(1+\\log h)^C.\n \\label{loc:tail-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $l\\geq0$, $b_v\\geq0$, and one common choice of $b_v$ works for the bounded number of expressions. Each expression is a polynomial combination of fixed bounded degree of the coordinates, fixed regular algebraic germs and fixed ordinary horizontal matrix germs, evaluated at $a$ and, in type <a href=\"#loc:paired\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:paired\">[loc:paired]</a>, at $aR$. Only a fixed finite list of such germs is used. Constants in the expressions may depend on the target, with the bounds displayed above.*\n\n*There are at most $Cd^C$ groups in the construction. This bound counts ordered conjugate tuples, not elements of the Galois group of a normal closure. The proposition asserts no nonidentity condition on an algebraic subvariety through $z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },

      "exact_statement": "There are fixed constants $C$ such that either $h\\leq Cd^C$, or there is one system of type <a href=\"#loc:single\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:single\">[loc:single]</a>, <a href=\"#loc:paired\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:paired\">[loc:paired]</a> or <a href=\"#loc:exception\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:exception\">[loc:exception]</a>, with <a href=\"#loc:base-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:base-equations\">[loc:base-equations]</a>, having the following properties.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nid-continuation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be an irreducible closed algebraic subvariety of the ambient affine space, containing $z$, on which $a$ is nonconstant. Let $D$ be a prime divisor on the normalization of $W$ lying over the generic point of a component of $W\\cap\\{a=0\\}$. If every expression in $\\mathscr E$ vanishes as a germ on this branch, then the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The base coordinates give actual algebraic projections to the fixed curve charts; the first projection $s$ is nonconstant and satisfies $x(s)=a$. In the paired case the second satisfies $x'(s')=aR$.*\n\n2.  *On a connected smooth algebraic open $W^\\circ$ all the identities continue along every path. At a fixed point of $W^\\circ$, continuation around a loop changes the horizontal transports by their pullback monodromy, while returning the algebraic coordinates to their original values.*\n\n3.  *Every operator pair that is independent at $z$ is independent at a general point of $W^\\circ$.*\n\n*The same conclusions hold if the pullbacks of all expressions vanish to infinite order along $D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

In the paired case the second satisfies $x'(s')=aR$.*\n\n2.  *On a connected smooth algebraic open $W^\\circ$ all the identities continue along every path. At a fixed point of $W^\\circ$, continuation around a loop changes the horizontal transports by their pullback monodromy, while returning the algebraic coordinates to their original values.*\n\n3.  *Every operator pair that is independent at $z$ is independent at a general point of $W^\\circ$.*\n\n*The same conclusions hold if the pullbacks of all expressions vanish to infinite order along $D$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-nid-matrix-span",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be the complex five-dimensional quadratic representation of [geo:five-space](#geo:five-space), and let $G=\\mathop{\\mathrm{SO}}(V)$.*\n\n1.  *If $b,v\\in V$ are nonzero, the function $g%\n      \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%\n      \\mapsto\\EndAccSupp{}\\langle b,gv\\rangle$ on $G$ is not constant.*\n\n2.  *The complex linear span of $G$ is $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_{\\mathbb C}(V)$. Consequently, if $b,c\\in V$, $p,q\\in V_1$, and $M_0:V_1\\to V$ is an isomorphism, the identities $$\\langle b,gM_0p\\rangle=\\langle c,gM_0q\\rangle\\qquad(g\\in G)$$ imply $b\\otimes p=c\\otimes q$, where the first tensor factors are identified with their duals by the quadratic pairing.*\n\n*The assertions remain valid if the identities are initially known only on a Zariski-dense monodromy subgroup.",
        "source_id": "PM-016.4"

        "exact_quote": "Fix $M$. Suppose at most $D$ polynomials in $M$ variables have degree at most $D\\geq2$ and affine coefficient height at most $H$.*\n\n1.  *Their ideal has a degree-compatible Gröbner basis of polynomially bounded degree and coefficient height at most $\\operatorname{poly}_M(D)(1+H)$. Each basis element has a representation in the original generators whose degree and coefficient height satisfy the same types of bounds. Normal-form reduction can be chosen linear. For an input of degree $T$, its representation by the remainder and the original generators has degree at most $T+\\Delta$, where $\\Delta=\\operatorname{poly}_M(D)$ is independent of $T$.*\n\n2.  *If a polynomial of degree $T$ belongs to the ideal, it has a certificate of membership of degree polynomial in $D+T$. The coefficient height of a certificate can be bounded polynomially in $D+T$ times one plus the heights of the given polynomial and generators.*\n\n3.  *Every irreducible component of the common zero set has degree polynomial in $D$, and admits set-theoretic defining equations whose number and degrees are polynomial in $D$ and whose affine coefficient heights are at most $\\operatorname{poly}_M(D)(1+H)$.*\n\n*All bounds are absolute and are unchanged by enlarging the number field. The ideals in <span class=\"upright\">(a)</span> and <span class=\"upright\">(b)</span> need not be radical.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Coarse moduli variety of principally polarized abelian surfaces** ([source](#intro:section)): Let $\mathcal A_2$ be the coarse moduli variety of principally polarized abelian surfaces.
- **Hodge-generic curve** ([source](#intro:section)): A curve in $\mathcal A_2$ is *Hodge generic* if it is contained in no proper Shimura-special subvariety.
- **Elliptic-square locus** ([source](#intro:section)): For a point $s\in\mathcal A_2(\overline{\mathbb Q})$, write $(A_s,\lambda_s)$ for its principally polarized abelian surface, and set $$\Sigma_{E^2}(C)= \bigl\{s\in C(\overline{\mathbb Q}): A_s\sim E^2 \text{ for an elliptic curve }E/\overline{\mathbb Q}\text{ without CM}\bigr\}.$$
- **Unpolarized geometric isogeny notation** ([source](#intro:section)): Here $\sim$ denotes isogeny over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, without any requirement that it respect the principal polarization.
- **Absolute affine logarithmic height and place weights** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a tuple $x=(x_1,\ldots,x_r)$ over a number field $F$, write $$h_{\mathrm{aff}}(x) =\sum_v w_v\log\max(1,\|x_1\|_v,\ldots,\|x_r\|_v), \qquad w_v=\frac{[F_v:\mathbb Q_v]}{[F:\mathbb Q]}.$$
- **Ordinary germ** ([source](#pm-h-8)): An *ordinary germ* below is a horizontal analytic solution in a rational frame in which the connection is regular at the specified center.
- **Trace-free self-adjoint operator system and trace pairing** ([source](#geo:operator-space)): Define $$\label{geo:operator-space} \mathcal P=\{D\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}(H):D^\dagger=D,\ \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_H(D)=0\}, \qquad \langle D,E\rangle=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_H(DE).$$
- **Saturated endomorphism-plane lattice** ([source](#pm-h-12)): At any target and prime, the plane lattice used for reduction is $$\label{geo:saturated-plane} \Pi_{t,\ell}=(F_t\otimes\mathbb Q_\ell)\cap\Lambda_\ell.$$
- **Dominating Hecke relation** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A *dominating relation* between closed curves $C_i,C_j$ means an irreducible algebraic curve contained in $(C_i\times C_j)\cap T_g$ with both projections dominant.
- **Degree parameter and shifted ample height** ([source](#stmt-ht-conventions)): Write $$d=\max\{2,[K(t):K]\},\qquad h=h_{[t_0]}(t)+c_0\geq 2.$$
- **Rosati quadratic size** ([source](#stmt-ht-endomorphisms)): For the principal Rosati involution $\dagger$, set $$Q_t(f)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{H_1}(ff^\dagger).$$
- **Horizontal back-transport** ([source](#pm-h-31)): In the rational frame of $\mathcal P_{\mathrm{dR}}$ let $Y(a)$ be horizontal back-transport from $t$ to $t_0$, normalized by $Y(0)=1$.
- **Full geometric order discriminant** ([source](#fin:disc-definition)): Throughout this section, the discriminant of this order means $$\label{fin:disc-definition} \Delta_s= \left\|\det\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{D_s/\mathbb Q}(e_i e_j)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq4}\right\|,$$
- **Elliptic-square special curve** ([source](#pm-h-44)): An $E^2$-special curve will mean a special curve whose generic rational endomorphism algebra is $\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\mathbb Q)$, as in (Daw and Orr 2022, sec. 6.2) [[DawOrr2022](#ref-DawOrr2022)].

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-operator-trace-gram-determinant | Gram determinant of the trace pairing in the displayed operator coordinates | = | $64$ | The displayed integral coordinate basis. |
| q-small-order-discriminant | Absolute discriminant of the full geometric endomorphism order | ≤ | $C(dh)^\kappa$ | Constants depend on the dimension and fixed data; target degree and height vary. |
| q-small-rosati-generators | Rosati quadratic sizes of full-algebra generators and the labeled plane pair | ≤ | $C(dh)^\kappa$ | Integral rationally spanning generators; they need not be an integral basis. |
| q-integral-projection-size-factor | Upper factor for quadratic size after integral symmetric trace-free projection | ≤ | $16$ | Clearing the displayed projection denominator. |
| q-global-plane-coordinate-height | Affine height of the labeled plane pair in the fixed rational frame | ≤ | $C(h+\log d)$ | All target degrees and heights; also simultaneous conjugates. |
| q-near-plane-coordinate-logarithms | Weighted positive coordinate logarithms of the labeled plane pair | ≤ | $C(1+\log h+\log d)$ | Normalized weights; arbitrary target extensions; fixed denominators and allowed transports. |
| q-local-convergence-exponent | Finite-prime convergence exponent | = | $\frac{\log p}{p-1}$ | Rational primes outside the fixed exceptional set. |
| q-retained-proximity-cutoff | Positive proximity logarithm at a retained spread-out prime | > | $2\beta_p$ | The valuation extends the usual rational-prime absolute value. |
| q-actual-branch-proximity-mass | Weighted proximity mass of nearby embeddings | ≥ | $c h-C\bigl(1+\log(dh)\bigr)^2$ | Constants depend on fixed disks and exceptional primes, uniformly over ramification. |
| q-ordered-tuple-group-count | Number of interpolation groups | ≤ | $Cd^C$ | The bound counts tuple values rather than normal-closure automorphisms. |
| q-group-target-height | Affine height of the grouped target tuple | ≤ | $Cd^C h$ | Fixed ambient dimension and fixed function list. |
| q-group-retained-mass | Weighted proximity mass in the selected group | ≥ | $\frac{h}{Cd^C}$ | The alternative already bounded-height case is excluded. |
| q-group-small-coordinate-logs | Weighted positive logarithms of grouped auxiliary coordinates | ≤ | $Cd^C(1+\log h)^C$ | The local sum precedes any degree-only height bound. |
| q-equation-coefficient-degree | Degree of a grouped equation coefficient polynomial | ≤ | $C(1+j)$ | Fixed-degree combinations of fixed germs; the series index varies. |
| q-equation-coefficient-height | Affine coefficient height of a grouped equation coefficient polynomial | ≤ | $C(1+j+d)^C(1+\log h)^C$ | Target-dependent constants are allowed within the stated coefficient bounds. |
| q-local-tail-evaluation | Absolute value of the grouped equation tail at the target | ≤ | $\|a\|_v^l\exp\bigl((1+l)b_v\bigr)$ | One common error choice serves the bounded collection of expressions. |
| q-selected-lift-height | Shifted ample height of a selected non-CM elliptic-square lift | ≤ | $C\max\{2,[K(t):K]\}^{\kappa}$ | Constants depend only on the fixed curve, covers, center, and auxiliary data. |
| q-full-order-orbit-lower-bound | Coarse-point field degree and Galois orbit cardinality | ≥ | $c\Delta_s^\delta$ | A fixed defining number field; full regular-trace order discriminant; finite omissions restored. |
| q-uniform-truncation-order | Truncation order giving poles on normalized boundary divisors | ≤ | $M_0(D)$ | Depends on the variety degree and fixed systems, independently of coefficient sizes. |
| q-projected-graph-boundary | Dimension of the closure of the projected graph boundary | ≤ | $k-2$ | The dimension bound concerns the projected boundary; the curve case means emptiness. |
| q-layer-condition-budget | Layered interpolation condition budget | < | $\binom{L+k}{k}$ | The left side is the displayed layered-condition expression; it is replaced by zero in the curve case. |
| q-prescribed-interpolation-ratio | Interpolation order | = | $cL$ | Any prescribed positive integral ratio, with degree polynomial in the controlled data and ratio. |

Then $$\\label{ht:near-coordinates}\n \\sum_{v\\in V}n_v\\log\\max\\{1,\\|P_1\\|_{v,\\mathrm{coord}},\n                                  \\|P_2\\|_{v,\\mathrm{coord}}\\}\n       \\leq C(1+\\log h+\\log d).$$ Both estimates hold for simultaneously conjugated data. They also hold for a fixed number of endomorphisms $F_j$ with $NF_j\\in\\mathcal O_t$ for a fixed positive integer $N$ and $Q_t(F_j)\\leq C(dh)^\\kappa$. If $\\phi:\\mathcal A_t\\to\\mathcal A_{t'}$ is a polarized isogeny of one of the fixed multipliers in [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list), write $I_t=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}(\\phi)$, so $I_t(D)=\\phi D\\phi^{-1}$. In particular the estimates hold for the transported pair $\\widehat{\\mathbf P}=I_t^{-1}\\mathbf P'$. The added choices in this last assertion have bounded relative field degree.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.4"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "loc:radius",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal S$ be a fixed finite set of rational primes containing the primes required for the models, frames, levels and fixed centered data. In particular, it contains the two auxiliary primes and the primes dividing the multipliers of [geo:hecke-list](#geo:hecke-list). It will also contain the finitely many primes specified in [loc:center-isogenies](#loc:center-isogenies) below. Outside $\\mathcal S$, the center, its affine chart, parameter and regular frame spread out smoothly, with an étale parameter and an abelian scheme over that chart. The inverse branch of each curve coordinate at $t_0$ consequently has integral power-series coefficients. Write $$\\beta_p=\\frac{\\log p}{p-1}.$$ At such a prime we retain only embeddings for which $t$ lies on that actual center branch and $$\\label{loc:radius}\n s_v:=-\\log|a|_v>2\\beta_p.$$ At the archimedean places and at primes in $\\mathcal S$, choose fixed sufficiently small disks on the actual branches. At a place where the center is potentially good, these disks are chosen to satisfy the good-reduction and convergence conditions below. At a place where it is not potentially good, [inc:bad-disks](#inc:bad-disks) excludes selected targets from a sufficiently small disk, so no such place is retained. Denote the resulting collection of nearby embeddings by $V_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.4"
        }
      ],

    {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "partially_effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-loc-groups",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed constants $C$ such that either $h\\leq Cd^C$, or there is one system of type <a href=\"#loc:single\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:single\">[loc:single]</a>, <a href=\"#loc:paired\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:paired\">[loc:paired]</a> or <a href=\"#loc:exception\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:exception\">[loc:exception]</a>, with <a href=\"#loc:base-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:base-equations\">[loc:base-equations]</a>, having the following properties. Its target is an algebraic point $z=(a,U)$ in an affine space of fixed dimension, and it holds at all embeddings in a weighted subcollection $V\\subset V_0$. Writing each scalar expression as $$E(a,U)=\\sum_{j\\geq0}a^j e_j(U),$$ the numerical and function hypotheses of hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n h_{\\mathrm{aff}}(z)&\\leq Cd^C h,\n &\\sum_{v\\in V}w_v\\log\\frac1{|a|_v}&\\geq\\frac{h}{Cd^C},\n \\label{loc:group-height-mass}\\\\\n \\sum_{v\\in V}w_v\\log^+\\|U\\|_v\n   &\\leq Cd^C(1+\\log h)^C,\n &\\deg e_j&\\leq C(1+j),\n \\label{loc:group-local-degree}\\\\\n h_{\\mathrm{aff}}(e_j)\n   &\\leq C(1+j+d)^C(1+\\log h)^C,\n \\label{loc:coefficient-height}\\\\\n \\left|\\sum_{j\\geq l}a^j e_j(U)\\right|_v\n   &\\leq |a|_v^l\\exp\\bigl((1+l)b_v\\bigr),\\notag\\\\\n \\sum_{v\\in V}w_v b_v&\\leq Cd^C(1+\\log h)^C.\n \\label{loc:tail-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $l\\geq0$, $b_v\\geq0$, and one common choice of $b_v$ works for the bounded number of expressions. Each expression is a polynomial combination of fixed bounded degree of the coordinates, fixed regular algebraic germs and fixed ordinary horizontal matrix germs, evaluated at $a$ and, in type <a href=\"#loc:paired\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:paired\">[loc:paired]</a>, at $aR$. Only a fixed finite list of such germs is used. Constants in the expressions may depend on the target, with the bounds displayed above.*\n\n*There are at most $Cd^C$ groups in the construction. This bound counts ordered conjugate tuples, not elements of the Galois group of a normal closure. The proposition asserts no nonidentity condition on an algebraic subvariety through $z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-016.4"
        }
      ],
